  private async runAgentRequest<T>(record: AgentRecord, request: () => Promise<T>): Promise<T> {
    assertAgentCanReceiveRequest(record);
    return await new Promise<T>((resolve, reject) => {
      const rejectForExit = (code: number | null, signal: NodeJS.Signals | null) => {
        const message = `Agent is not running: ${record.name} (exit code=${code} signal=${signal})`;
        record.state = "stopped";
        record.lastError = record.lastError ?? `exit code=${code} signal=${signal}`;
        record.updatedAt = nowIso();
        reject(new RuntimeHttpError(409, message));
      };
      const rejectForError = (error: Error) => {
        const message = `Agent process error: ${error.message}`;
        record.state = "error";
        record.lastError = message;
        record.updatedAt = nowIso();
        reject(new RuntimeHttpError(500, message));
      };
      const cleanup = () => {
        record.child.off("exit", rejectForExit);
        record.child.off("error", rejectForError);
      };

      record.child.once("exit", rejectForExit);
      record.child.once("error", rejectForError);
      suppressClosedPipeAcpWriteConsoleError(request).then(resolve, reject).finally(cleanup);
    });
  }

  async startAgent(input: StartAgentInput) {
    const accountId = input.accountId?.trim() || null;
    if (this.workerPool.isAccountFenced(accountId)) {
      throw new RuntimeHttpError(409, `Account ${accountId} is quiesced for a lifecycle operation.`);
    }
    const name = input.name?.trim() || "pending-agent";
    if (this.startingAgentAccounts.has(name)) {
      const message = `Agent is already starting: ${name}`;
      emitNamedEvent({
        kind: "runtime.agent_start_coalesced",
        workerId: name,
      });
      throw new RuntimeHttpError(409, message);
    }

## Claude thinking · 2026-10-03T07:35:29.288Z

The actual root cause seems clear now: the Codex account hit its usage limit at 07:24:10, pushing the goal into "limited" state. When the user pressed Resume, OmniHarness sent a resume request to codex-acp, which hangs since codex app-server can't start a turn due to the limit and never replies — and without a timeout, that request just hangs indefinitely.

I'm trying to figure out whether "request_failed" surfaces from a stream resync or from a runtime timeout, possibly electron's 30-second limit. Let me check what electron.ts does around the generic request handling.

export function createElectronRuntimeAPIs(options: {
  target: ElectronRuntimeTarget;
  bridge?: ElectronNativeBridge | null;
  timeoutMs?: number;
}): RuntimeAPIs {
  const bridge = resolveBridge(options.bridge);
  const timeoutMs = options.timeoutMs ?? 30_000;

  async function invoke(
    type: ElectronBridgeRequest["type"],
    payload?: unknown,
    target = options.target,
  ) {
    const id = String(++requestSequence);
    const result = await Promise.race([
      bridge.invokeRuntime({ id, type, target, payload }),
      new Promise<never>((_, reject) => setTimeout(() => reject({
        code: "surface.bridge_failed",
        message: "Electron bridge request timed out.",
        surface: "electron",
      }), timeoutMs)),
    ]);
    if (!result || result.id !== id || result.type !== type) {
      throw {
        code: "surface.bridge_failed",
        message: "Electron bridge returned an invalid response.",
        surface: "electron",
      } satisfies RuntimeApiError;
    }

## Claude thinking · 2026-10-03T07:35:36.249Z

That 30-second Electron timeout matches the error appearing after the button press, while a browser would just hang indefinitely waiting. The outbox is also stuck—rev 9 was claimed but revs 10-12 are still pending and never published, suggesting the drain process got blocked after dispatch and the goal.updated events for those revisions never fired.

Now I'm wondering why codex-acp hangs on the session/goal resume call. Since Codex is usage-limited until Oct 9, the adapter is probably invoking a thread/goal resume that starts a turn in app-server and just waits—I should check the bridge lock file or find where the codex-acp adapter source actually lives.

## Claude thinking · 2026-10-03T07:36:01.784Z

I'm tracing the resume logic step by step: it waits for a goal response, then a matching update notification, then turn start or a grace period, then completion. If codex's usage is limited, the goal status might stay "budgetLimited" instead of "active," which would make the code wait indefinitely for a goal-update event that never arrives.

Alternatively, the turn might fail immediately due to usage limits before routing captures it, leaving the adapter stuck awaiting turn-started forever. Either way, there's no timeout on this resume call, so when Codex hits usage limits the UI just waits until Electron's bridge timeout kicks in and shows a generic error — I want to check whether both stuck sessions correspond to Codex sessions that hit usage limits, so I should inspect that session stream for the limit event.

## Claude thinking · 2026-10-03T07:36:15.156Z

Both Codex sessions on the account have hit the usage limit until Oct 9, which is why resume hangs. I'm also noticing the OmniHarness DB still claims "pursuing" even though Codex is actually blocked—a state lie—and outbox events for revisions 10-12 remain unpublished since the dispatch hang prevents drainPending from ever running.

Since the per-run dispatch queue is blocked, retries on these runs will also hang until the runtime or agent process restarts—retrying won't help while the account remains limited. The user's question "what happened? it should work" reads as analysis, so I'll explain the root cause and propose fixes (adding a timeout to the extension call so it's treated as a transport failure, and having resume report "limited" explicitly) without implementing changes myself, keeping the answer concise.

<!-- codex-event:{"kind":"final","timestamp":"2026-10-03T07:36:17.852Z","phase":null} -->
## Claude · 2026-10-03T07:36:17.852Z

I traced it. Your resume didn't fail on our side, and it can't succeed right now: the Codex account behind that session is out of usage, and the Codex adapter never answers a resume request in that state.

**Timeline for d06affd28dec**
- **07:24:10 UTC.** Codex stopped mid-goal with "You've hit your usage limit… try again at Oct 9th, 2026 11:11 PM." The goal moved to `limited`.
- **07:33:53.** You pressed Resume. The server saved it right away (`run_goal_operations` shows the resume committed, and the goal is now revision 12, `pursuing`). It then sent `_session/goal {action: "resume"}` to the Codex worker.
- **The call never returned.** The event log has `acp.method_started` for `_session/goal`, with no completion and no failure after it. The UI request timed out after 30s (the Electron bridge timeout in `src/runtime-api/electron.ts:103`), and that timeout produces the generic "The goal could not be updated" message.

Session 9d1e7dd343df hit the same limit at 07:24:28, and its resume at 07:31:10 is stuck the same way.

**Why it hangs**
- In the codex-acp adapter, `runGoalSet` waits for Codex to confirm the goal and then for a turn to start and finish. A usage-limited account never starts that turn, so the wait has no end.
- On our side, `goal-acp.ts:111` → `invokeAgentAcpMethod` → `manager.invokeAcpMethod` has no timeout anywhere, so the server waits forever too.

**What this has left broken**
1. **The goal shows the wrong state.** It says `pursuing`, but Codex is still limited.
2. **Pressing Retry won't help.** Goal controls run one at a time per run (`GoalControlDispatchCoordinator.enqueue`), so new actions on these two runs wait behind the hung call until the worker or runtime restarts.
3. **Goal updates have stopped reaching the UI.** The request is stuck before `goalOutboxDispatcher.drainPending()`, so revisions 10–12 are still `pending` in `run_goal_outbox`. The revision 9 row is also stuck in `claimed` with an expired claim.

**What you can do now:** the session can't really resume until the limit resets on Oct 9 at 23:11, or until it runs on a different Codex account. Restarting the worker will clear the hung call.

**Proposed fix (not applied, since you asked what happened):**
- Put a timeout on the `_session/goal` call. When it expires, treat it as `deferred` and keep the goal in its real state instead of hanging.
- When the provider's goal is `limited` because the account is out of usage, refuse Resume up front with a specific message (something like "Codex usage limit reached until Oct 9, 23:11") instead of the generic one.
- Add a lifecycle scenario under `tests/lifecycle/scenarios/` with a mock agent that never answers `_session/goal`.

I left the existing uncommitted changes in the working tree alone. They look like separate work on the stuck-worker reaper. Want me to implement the fix?

## Claude thinking · 2026-10-03T07:53:27.915Z

The resume actually completed. Tracing timestamps, 9d1e shows a goal starting at 07:31:10, then another one starting at 07:36:16 before completing at 07:36:17, so it seems like only one completion got logged despite two starts; d06a similarly started at 07:33:53 with a later event around 07:38:57.

This pattern — a second start roughly 5 minutes after the first, completing almost instantly — strongly suggests a request timeout and retry. Node's undici fetch defaults to a 300-second headers timeout, which matches both gaps almost exactly (07:33:53+5:04=07:38:57, 07:31:10+5:06=07:36:16). So the original HTTP call to the bridge hung for 5 minutes, hit UND_ERR_HEADERS_TIMEOUT, and the retry logic succeeded within a second. I'm now trying to figure out why that first call hung in the first place — possibly related to the thread/goal/set response status at the time.

But worker status stayed idle until 07:41:45, which is odd if a turn actually started. I should check the worker stream entries for d06a between 07:33:53 and 07:39 to see if a turn ran and possibly failed immediately due to a stale rate-limit error cached in app-server.

## Claude thinking · 2026-10-03T07:53:43.857Z

So the first goal call hung silently for the full five minutes with no output, confirming it was stuck waiting rather than failing immediately — the undici default headersTimeout of 300s on the control plane's fetch likely killed the request to the bridge, triggering the retry that succeeded right away.

I should check the codex-tui.log for timing on the thread/goal/set call, and also test the undici headersTimeout theory — maybe the fetch failure is a HeadersTimeoutError, which would be recoverable and trigger a retry. It's also possible the bridge's own Node HTTP server has a default 300s requestTimeout that's killing the request independently of undici, which would produce the same retry-after-5-minutes pattern either way.

I'm noticing a gap in d06's event stream between 07:29:50 and 07:38:58 — the turn kept running, but nothing forwarded to OmniHarness until the retry at 07:38:57, after which events resumed flowing normally with compaction completing at 07:41:24. I'm wondering whether this relates to how the adapter routes turn notifications — specifically whether captureTurnRoutings only forwards events to a session while a prompt is actively tracked. I want to check the adapter's logging and how it dispatches notifications for turns that weren't started through the normal prompt path, so I'm going to grep for captureTurnRoutings and the notification handler to see how turn events get published to session updates.

## Claude thinking · 2026-10-03T07:55:00.848Z

That subscription persists since subscribe just replaces `current`, so with promptNotificationsActive false, a turn resumed via goal (not through a prompt) only passes session-scoped events like goal updates and usage—item notifications get dropped, which explains the silence in those first 5 minutes. Then the second `_session/goal` call triggers startGoalContinuationIfCurrent into startNewTurnFromEx...

Still tracing the call chain...

The continuation prompt likely steered into the same existing turn rather than creating a new one, since Codex logs show only a single turn id spanning the whole period. The 5-minute delay probably comes from a default request timeout (like Node's 300s headers/request timeout) in the bridge layer, with a retry accidentally resolving it, while the UI error was just the client giving up earlier than the server. So my takeaway: the resume actually did work promptly on the Codex side — I was wrong to think otherwise.

The real issue is that the goal-resume call doesn't reply until the entire resumed turn completes, so OmniHarness just waits indefinitely while the UI's 30s timeout fires a false failure. Worse, because the resumed turn wasn't started via a prompt, its item events weren't forwarded, meaning the tool calls Codex ran during that first ~5 minutes never made it into the transcript — only after the automatic retry attached via the "continuation prompt" path did output start flowing again, leaving a real gap in the recorded history.

For a fix, I'm weighing two approaches: either make OmniHarness not block on the provider ACK — using a short timeout and treating the `thread goal updated status active` event as confirmation instead — or patch the codex-acp adapter itself so resumed turns don't wait for full completion and don't drop their events. I should check whether OmniHarness already has a patching mechanism for codex-acp, since there's a system_message_renderer.patch in the repo that suggests it does.

## Claude · 2026-10-03T07:55:13.968Z

You're right, and my first answer was wrong. Your usage reset worked, and so did the resume. The error message was a false failure.

**What actually happened (d06affd28dec)**
- **07:33:53.** You pressed Resume. Codex accepted it immediately and started working. Its own logs (`cloudcaptions/.omniharness/cli-home/codex/sqlite/logs_2.sqlite`) show turn `01a100af…` starting that second and running tools ever since. It's the same turn that's still running now.
- **The resume request never got an answer.** The Codex adapter (codex-acp 1.13) doesn't reply to a goal resume until the whole resumed turn finishes, and for a goal that's the entire job. OmniHarness waits on that reply with no timeout, so the request just sat there.
- **The UI gave up after 30s** (the Electron request timeout) and showed "The goal could not be updated." The goal was fine; we just never got a reply.

**A second problem: you lost about 5 minutes of transcript**
- The adapter only forwards a turn's output when the turn started from a prompt. A turn started by a goal resume doesn't count, so nothing Codex did from 07:33:53 to 07:38:57 reached OmniHarness. That's why the session looked dead.
- At 07:38:57 the request hit a 5-minute HTTP timeout and retried on its own. The retry went through a different path in the adapter that sends a continuation prompt. That attached to the running turn, and output began flowing at 07:38:58.
- The tool calls from that first window are missing from the conversation, though their effects on disk are real.

Session 9d1e7dd343df went through exactly the same thing: resumed at 07:31:10, retried at 07:36:16, output after that. Both sessions are working normally now, so you don't need to do anything.

**Proposed fix (on our side, since codex-acp is a third-party package):**
1. Stop holding the resume request open until the provider replies. Give `_session/goal` a short wait (around 10–15s). If the adapter hasn't answered by then but its goal update has reported `status: active`, treat the resume as applied and return success.
2. After dispatching a resume to Codex, attach a listener for the running turn right away (the same thing the retry did by accident), so the first minutes of output aren't dropped.
3. Add a lifecycle scenario under `tests/lifecycle/scenarios/` with a mock agent that starts a turn on resume and never replies.

Want me to implement it?
